data-jigconfig="allHeadingLevels: ['h2'],smoothScroll: false" itemprop="text"><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Summary" itemprop="description"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Summary_">Summary</h2><div><h4 class="inline">Clinical characteristics.</h4><p>The phenotypic spectrum of ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T), a multisystem disorder, is a continuum ranging from classic A-T at the severe end and variant A-T at the milder end. Nonetheless, distinguishing between classic A-T and variant A-T on this spectrum helps understand differences in disease course, rate of progression, and life expectancy.</p><p><i>Classic A-T</i> is characterized by childhood onset of progressive neurologic manifestations (initially cerebellar ataxia, followed typically by extrapyramidal involvement and peripheral sensorimotor neuropathy), immunodeficiency (variably associated with abnormalities of humoral immunity, cellular immunity, or combined immune deficiency), pulmonary disease (resulting from recurrent infections, immune deficiency, aspiration, interstitial lung disease, and neurologic abnormalities), and increased risk of malignancy. Although it is generally accepted that intellectual disability is not common in A-T, disturbances in cerebellar as well as non-cerebellar brain areas and networks may result in cognitive deficits. Increased sensitivity to ionizing radiation (x-ray and gamma ray) can result in severe side effects from such treatments. Life expectancy is significantly reduced due to cancer, pulmonary disease, and infections.</p><p><i>Variant A-T</i> has a significantly milder disease course. While cerebellar ataxia can be absent, extrapyramidal movement disorders are common (typically dystonia and dystonic tremor) and most individuals have manifestations of axonal sensorimotor polyneuropathy. In contrast to classic A-T, immune function is generally normal, respiratory infections are not increased, and pulmonary disease is not a major feature. However, risk of developing malignancies is increased, particularly in premenopausal females who have an increased risk of developing breast cancer and hematologic malignancies.</p></div><div><h4 class="inline">Diagnosis/testing.</h4><p>The diagnosis of A-T is established in a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> with suggestive findings and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>ATM</i> identified by <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a>. Of note, newborn screening (NBS) for severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) that relies on the identification of reduced T-cell receptor excision circle (TREC) levels in blood spots most likely identifies about 50% of children with classic A-T, who require immediate subspecialty evaluation at a center with expertise in the diagnosis of SCID, its causes, and its treatment.</p></div><div><h4 class="inline">Management.</h4><p><i>Treatment of manifestations:</i> Supportive care to improve quality of life, maximize function, and reduce complications ideally involves multidisciplinary care by specialists in (pediatric) neurology, pulmonology, immunology, pediatrics (for children) and internal medicine (for adults), rehabilitation medicine, and professionals in physical therapy, speech-language therapy, occupational therapy, and nutrition. For specific issues care may be provided by specialists in oncology, medical genetics, endocrinology, orthopedics, dermatology, mental health, and social work.</p><p><i>Surveillance:</i> Routine evaluations by treating clinicians is necessary to monitor existing manifestations, the individual's response to supportive care, and the emergence of new manifestations. Recommended surveillance is the same for individuals with classic A-T and variant A-T, with the exception that individuals who do not have evidence of lung disease at the time of initial diagnosis do not require annual screening for pulmonary disease.</p><p><i>Agents/circumstances to avoid:</i> Rubella vaccination should be avoided in individuals with severe immunodeficiency as it can possibly increase the risk for granulomas; ionizing radiation (x-ray and gamma ray) is contraindicated, due to increased sensitivity; and radiation therapy is contraindicated because increased radiosensitivity may lead to very severe complications.</p><p><i>Evaluation of relatives at risk:</i> It is appropriate to offer <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> for the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants to apparently asymptomatic older and younger at-risk relatives of an affected individual in order to identify as early as possible sibs with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants who would benefit from prompt initiation of treatment of manifestations of A-T, surveillance for malignancy, and awareness of agents/circumstances to avoid; and family members who are <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and who would benefit from age-appropriate intensified surveillance for breast cancer.</p><p><i>Therapies under investigation:</i> Use of intra-erythrocyte dexamethasone; the effects of nicotinamide riboside (a form of vitamin B<sub>3</sub>) on ataxia scores and immunoglobulin levels; <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene-therapy/">gene therapy</a> with antisense oligonucleotides in a single case study; effect of allogenic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation on neurologic functioning.</p></div><div><h4 class="inline">Genetic counseling.</h4><p>A-T is caused by <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>ATM</i> and inherited in an <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner. If both parents are known to be <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of inheriting biallelic pathogenic variants and having A-T, a 50% chance of inheriting one pathogenic variant and being heterozygous, and a 25% chance of inheriting neither of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants. Although individuals heterozygous for an <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variant are not at risk for A-T, their risk of developing cancer is increased compared to that of the general population (in particular, heterozygous females have an increased risk of developing breast cancer). Once the <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants have been identified in an affected family member, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a> testing for at-risk relatives and prenatal and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> for A-T are possible.</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.GeneReview_Scope"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_GeneReview_Scope_"><i>GeneReview</i> Scope</h2><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas" class="table"><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Phenotypic Spectrum</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Severity</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2" colspan="4" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:center;vertical-align:middle;">Key Differences In:</th></tr><tr><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_1" colspan="1" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Neurologic decline</th><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Immunodeficiency &#x00026; pulmonary disease</th><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Age at typical first occurrence of malignancy</th><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_4" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Life expectancy</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Classic A-T</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Childhood onset</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually present</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Childhood (median age: 12.5 yrs)</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Significantly reduced due to cancer, pulmonary disease, &#x00026; infections</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Variant A-T</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Less progressive</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Typically absent</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Adulthood</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_2 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_2_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Longer than in classic A-T</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">A-T = ataxia-telangiectasia</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Diagnosis"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Diagnosis_">Diagnosis</h2><p>No consensus clinical diagnostic criteria for ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T) have been published.</p><p>The two scenarios in which A-T may be considered are <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Scenario_1_Abnormal">abnormal newborn screening</a> for severe combined immunodeficiency and a <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Scenario_2_Symptomat">symptomatic proband</a>.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Scenario_1_Abnormal"><h3>Scenario 1: Abnormal Newborn Screening for Severe Combined Immunodeficiency</h3><p>Newborn screening (NBS) for severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID), a severe but treatable immunologic disorder, relies on the identification of reduced T-cell receptor excision circle (TREC) levels in blood spots.</p><p><b>Classic A-T</b>. Newborns with classic A-T (who are still asymptomatic and undiagnosed) may have low TREC levels comparable to the TREC levels of newborns with SCID and, thus, may have a positive NBS. NBS for SCID most likely identifies about 50% of children with classic A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mallott.2013.540">Mallott et al 2013</a>]. Note: Infants with classic A-T who have an abnormal NBS result also demonstrate the <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Classic_AT">laboratory findings suggestive of classic A-T</a>.</p><p><b>Variant A-T.</b> Since variant A-T is not associated with immunodeficiencies, NBS for SCID does not detect variant A-T.</p><p>Note: This chapter specifically focuses on A-T; for potential causes of decreased TREC levels in NBS other than classic A-T and SCID, see <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.puck.2019.241">Puck [2019]</a>.</p><p>Newborns with an abnormal NBS for SCID <b>require immediate subspecialty immunology evaluation</b> at a center with expertise in the diagnosis of SCID, its genetic causes, and its treatment protocols, including hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT). (See Management, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Evaluations_Followin">Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</a>.)</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Scenario_2_Symptomat"><h3>Scenario 2: Symptomatic Proband with Findings Suggestive of A-T</h3><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Classic_AT"><h4>Classic A-T</h4><p>
<b>Clinical findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Cerebellar ataxia (the presenting feature in most individuals)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Extrapyramidal movement disorders (chorea, myoclonus, dystonia, tremor)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Dysarthria</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eye movement disorders (gaze-evoked nystagmus, oculomotor apraxia&#x000a0;*)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Oculocutaneous telangiectasias&#x000a0;*</div></li></ul><p>*&#x000a0;In most individuals with classic A-T, abnormal eye movements and telangiectasias only occur some years after disease onset. Motor and sensory neuropathy generally develop at the end of the first decade of life.</p><p>
<b>Laboratory findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Serum concentration of alpha-fetoprotein (AFP)</b> is the most important blood biomarker in individuals with classic A-T. In all individuals with classic A-T, serum AFP is elevated (&#x0003e;10 ng/mL, range: 200-1,000 ng/mL) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.waldmann.1972.1112">Waldmann &#x00026; Mcintire 1972</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.straypedersen.2007.375">Stray-Pedersen et al 2007</a>]. Note: Because serum AFP concentrations are high at birth in all persons and only reach normal values around age two years, it is unreliable as a diagnostic marker in young children [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schieving.2014.243">Schieving et al 2014</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>
<b>Immunologic</b>
</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Serum levels of immunoglobulins.</b> Especially IgA, IgG, and IgG subclasses (in particular IgG2 and IgG4) are decreased [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>]. Approximately 10% of individuals with classic A-T have IgA and IgG deficiency with normal or raised IgM concentration, the so-called hyper IgM <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.noordzij.2009.448">Noordzij et al 2009</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Immunophenotype.</b> Numbers of B and T cells, in particular na&#x000ef;ve CD4<sup>+</sup> and CD8 cells, may be decreased [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.driessen.2013.1367">Driessen et al 2013</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Lymphocyte functional tests.</b> Impairment of lymphocyte function can be present in individuals with A-T. In particular, a restricted response to antigens and abnormal B-cell class switching may be found [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.staples.2008.214">Staples et al 2008</a>].</div></li></ul></li></ul><p><b>Brain MRI findings.</b> The classic finding, cerebellar atrophy affecting both the cerebellar vermis and the hemispheres [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.tavani.2003.315">Tavani et al 2003</a>], is usually not present when the first clinical manifestations occur [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lin.2014.119">Lin et al 2014</a>].</p><p><b>Family history</b> is consistent with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> inheritance (e.g., affected sibs and/or parental <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/consanguinity/">consanguinity</a>). A history of cancer in a sib or parent (particularly of breast cancer in the mother of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> and/or a severe reaction to chemotherapy and/or radiation in the mother) may suggest the diagnosis of A-T. Absence of a known family history of A-T or cancer does not preclude the diagnosis.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Variant_AT"><h4>Variant A-T</h4><p>
<b>Clinical findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Cerebellar ataxia</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Extrapyramidal movement disorders (dystonia, chorea, myoclonus, tremor)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Dysarthria</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Peripheral neuropathy; anterior horn disease in a minority of individuals</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Cancer in a young individual with an apparently static neurologic (motor) disorder ("cerebral palsy")</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Laboratory findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Serum concentration of AFP.</b> Although serum AFP levels are generally increased in variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.waldmann.1972.1112">Waldmann &#x00026; Mcintire 1972</a>], they are not as high as those observed in classic A-T and can eventually become normal [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019b.e19">van Os et al 2019b</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Immunologic.</b> No suggestive immunologic findings</div></li></ul><p><b>Brain MRI findings.</b> Although brain MRI shows atrophy of both the cerebellar vermis and hemispheres in most individuals with variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.tavani.2003.315">Tavani et al 2003</a>], it can also be normal [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Family history</b> is consistent with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> inheritance (e.g., affected sibs and/or parental <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/consanguinity/">consanguinity</a>). A history of cancer in a sib or parent (particularly of breast cancer in the mother of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> and/or a severe reaction to chemotherapy and/or radiation in the mother) may suggest the diagnosis of A-T. Absence of a known family history of A-T or cancer does not preclude the diagnosis.</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Establishing_the_Dia"><h3>Establishing the Diagnosis</h3><p>The diagnosis of A-T <b>is established</b> in a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> with <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Scenario_2_Symptomat">suggestive findings</a> and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic (or <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/likely-pathogenic/">likely pathogenic</a>) variants in <i>ATM</i> identified by <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> (see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas1">Table 1</a>).</p><p>Note: (1) Per American College of Medical Genetics and Genomics&#x000a0;/ Association for Molecular Pathology variant interpretation guidelines, the terms "<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>" and "<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/likely-pathogenic/">likely pathogenic</a> variant" are synonymous in a clinical setting, meaning that both are considered diagnostic and can be used for clinical decision making [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.richards.2015.405">Richards et al 2015</a>]. Reference to "pathogenic variants" in this <i>GeneReview</i> is understood to include likely pathogenic variants. (2) Identification of <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> <i>ATM</i> variants of <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a> (or of one known <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variant and one <i>ATM</i> variant of uncertain significance) itself does not establish or rule out the diagnosis.</p><p>Molecular genetic testing approaches can include a combination of <b><a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-targeted testing</b> (single-gene testing, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/multigene-panel/">multigene panel</a>) and <b>comprehensive</b>
<b><a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing</b> (<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome-sequencing/">exome sequencing</a>, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a>). Gene-targeted testing requires that the clinician determine which gene(s) are likely involved (see <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Option_1">Option 1</a>), whereas comprehensive genomic testing does not (see <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Option_2">Option 2</a>).</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Option_1"><h4>Option 1</h4><p><b>Single-<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> testing.</b> Sequence analysis of <i>ATM</i> is performed first to detect small <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a>, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/nonsense-variant/">nonsense</a>, and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/splice-site/">splice site</a> variants and intragenic deletions/insertions. Note: Depending on the sequencing method used, single-<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exon/">exon</a>, multiexon, or whole-gene deletions/duplications may not be detected. If only one or no variant is detected by the sequencing method used, the next step is to perform gene-targeted <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion-duplication-analysis/">deletion/duplication analysis</a> to detect exon and whole-gene deletions or duplications.</p><p><b>A <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/multigene-panel/">multigene panel</a></b> (e.g., for ataxias, immunodeficiency, or cancer predisposition) that includes <i>ATM</i> and other genes of interest (see <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Differential_Diagnos">Differential Diagnosis</a>) is most likely to identify the genetic cause of the condition while limiting identification of incidental findings (such as pathogenic variants and variants of <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a> in genes that do not explain the underlying <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>). Note: (1) The genes included in the panel and the diagnostic <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sensitivity/">sensitivity</a> of the testing used for each <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> vary by laboratory and are likely to change over time. (2) Some multigene panels may include genes not associated with the condition discussed in this <i>GeneReview</i>. (3) In some laboratories, panel options may include a custom laboratory-designed panel and/or custom phenotype-focused <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome/">exome</a> analysis that includes genes specified by the clinician. (4) Methods used in a panel may include <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a>, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion-duplication-analysis/">deletion/duplication analysis</a>, and/or other non-sequencing-based tests.</p><p>For an introduction to multigene panels click <a href="/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Multigene_Panels">here</a>. More detailed information for clinicians ordering genetic tests can be found <a href="/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Multigene_Panels_FAQs">here</a>.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Option_2"><h4>Option 2</h4><p><b>Comprehensive</b>
<b><a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing</b> does not require the clinician to determine which <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> is likely involved. <b>Exome sequencing</b> is most commonly used; <b><a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a></b> is also possible. Note: Unlike <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome-sequencing/">exome sequencing</a>, genome sequencing can identify variants outside of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/coding-region/">coding region</a>. Although most confirmed pathogenic <i>ATM</i> variants are within exons [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.taylor.2015.199">Taylor et al 2015</a>], several pathogenic variants have been detected in the noncoding region of <i>ATM</i> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcconville.1996.320">McConville et al 1996</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.castellv_bel.1999.156">Castellv&#x000ed;-Bel et al 1999</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.fi_vet.2019.1713">Fi&#x000e9;vet et al 2019</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.cremin.2020.4004">Cremin et al 2020</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.klee.2021.498">Klee et al 2021</a>].</p><p>For an introduction to comprehensive <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> testing click <a href="/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Comprehensive_Genomic_Testing">here</a>. More detailed information for clinicians ordering genomic testing can be found <a href="/books/n/gene/app5/#app5.Comprehensive_Genomic_Testing_1">here</a>.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 1. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Molecular Genetic Testing</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Gene&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Method</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Proportion of Pathogenic Variants&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup> Identified by Method</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>ATM</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Sequence analysis&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">90%-95%&#x000a0;<sup>4,&#x000a0;5</sup></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Deletion/<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/duplication/">duplication</a> analysis&#x000a0;<sup>6</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_1_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">5%-10%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.1"><p class="no_margin">See <a href="/books/NBK26468/#ataxia-telangiectas.molgen.TA">Table A. Genes and Databases</a> for <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/locus/">locus</a> and protein.</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.2"><p class="no_margin">See <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Molecular_Genetics">Molecular Genetics</a> for information on variants detected in this <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>.</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.3"><p class="no_margin">Sequence analysis detects variants that are benign, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/likely-benign/">likely benign</a>, of <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/uncertain-significance/">uncertain significance</a>, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/likely-pathogenic/">likely pathogenic</a>, or pathogenic. Variants may include <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a>, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/nonsense-variant/">nonsense</a>, and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/splice-site/">splice site</a> variants and small intragenic deletions/duplications/insertions; typically, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exon/">exon</a> or whole-<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> deletions/duplications are not detected. For issues to consider in interpretation of <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a> results, click <a href="/books/n/gene/app2/">here</a>.</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.4"><p class="no_margin">Data derived from the subscription-based professional view of Human Gene Mutation Database [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.stenson.2020.1197">Stenson et al 2020</a>]</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.5"><p class="no_margin">Several deep <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/intronic/">intronic</a> variants outside of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exon/">exon</a> and splice junction regions typically included by standard sequencing have been observed, including a deep intronic <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/founder-variant/">founder variant</a> detected in the United Kingdom (<a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar">c.5763-1050A&#x0003e;G</a>, formerly known as 5762ins137) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcconville.1996.320">McConville et al 1996</a>].</p></div></dd><dt>6. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.1.6"><p class="no_margin">Gene-targeted <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion-duplication-analysis/">deletion/duplication analysis</a> detects intragenic deletions or duplications. Methods used may include a range of techniques such as <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/quantitative-pcr/">quantitative PCR</a>, long-range PCR, multiplex ligation-dependent probe amplification (MLPA), and a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a>-targeted microarray designed to detect single-<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exon/">exon</a> deletions or duplications. Alternatively, deletion/duplication analysis by using read depth or detecting breakpoints in <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/exome/">exome</a> and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genome-sequencing/">genome sequencing</a> data is also possible.</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Clinical_Characteris"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Clinical_Characteris_">Clinical Characteristics</h2><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Clinical_Description"><h3>Clinical Description</h3><p>Ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T) is often described has having a "classic A-T" <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> and a "variant A-T" phenotype; however, these phenotypes are more of a continuum ranging from classic A-T at the severe end to variant A-T at the milder end. Nonetheless, distinguishing between classic A-T and variant- A-T on this phenotypic spectrum helps understand differences in disease course, rate of progression, and life expectancy (see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas2">Table 2</a>) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al 2017b</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.levy.2018.1238">Levy &#x00026; Lang 2018</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.amirifar.2019.277">Amirifar et al 2019</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2020b.830">van Os et al 2020b</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021b.450">Veenhuis et al 2021b</a>].</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 2. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Comparison of Classic A-T and Variant A-T by Select Features</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Feature</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Classic A-T</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Variant A-T</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" rowspan="6" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cerebellar ataxia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+, &#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Extrapyramidal movement disorder</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+, &#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Peripheral neuropathy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+, &#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dysarthria</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dysphagia/feeding/nutrition issues</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x000b1;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="2" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Oculomotor apraxia</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+, &#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Increased susceptibility to malignancy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Abnormal cognition &#x00026; behavior</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x000b1;</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">?</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Immunodeficiency</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ab</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Infection</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x000b1;</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ab</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pulmonary disease</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ab</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" colspan="2" scope="rowgroup" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine abnormalities</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Growth failure</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">++</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">?</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Abnormal puberty</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Insulin resistance</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x000b1;</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_1" colspan="3" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Telangiectasias</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_2_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">+, &#x000b1;, &#x02212;</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">Based on <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2014.297">Hoche et al [2014]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017a.680">van Os et al [2017a]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.levy.2018.1238">Levy &#x00026; Lang [2018]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.amirifar.2019.277">Amirifar et al [2019]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2019.225">Hoche et al [2019]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al [2019]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2020b.830">van Os et al [2020b]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021b.450">Veenhuis et al [2021b]</a></p></div></dd><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">++ = always present; + = usually present; &#x000b1; = sometimes present; &#x02212; rarely present; Ab = absent; ? = although this feature has not been systematically studied in individuals with variant A-T, the authors feel that this is very uncommon in these individuals</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Classic_AT_1"><h4>Classic A-T</h4><p><b>Neurologic.</b> In most children with classic A-T, cerebellar ataxia is the presenting manifestation observed after the child starts to sit and walk [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.boder.1958.526">Boder &#x00026; Sedgwick 1958</a>]. Infants show postural instability when sitting or standing. Although children start to walk at a normal age, their gait does not improve and remains wobbly.</p><p>During the first years after disease onset, motor abnormalities may appear to be stable because the natural acquisition of motor milestones in young children may appear to "compensate" for the (progressive) disease course [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.levy.2018.1238">Levy &#x00026; Lang 2018</a>].</p><p>Children have balance problems when they walk and show a rapid broad-based gait, with backward posturing of the arms and hands as a finding of dystonia.</p><p>In addition, most children with classic A-T have extrapyramidal involvement such as dystonia, choreoathetosis, myoclonic jerks, and tremor. In the second or third decade, parkinsonism can occur [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.levy.2018.1238">Levy &#x00026; Lang 2018</a>].</p><p>After about age eight years children begin to show clinical signs of peripheral neuropathy, manifesting as a progressive sensorimotor neuropathy, decreased or loss of deep tendon reflexes, distal muscle weakness and atrophy, and impaired vibration sense [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2007.117">Verhagen et al 2007</a>].</p><p>After about age ten years most children with classic A-T require use a wheelchair to compensate for the cerebellar ataxia and extrapyramidal and peripheral nervous manifestations.</p><p>With disease progression, speech problems occur in all individuals. Despite dysarthria, oral communication generally remains possible [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021b.450">Veenhuis et al 2021b</a>].</p><p>Dysphagia, which is common in young adults, makes eating frustrating and exhausting [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2000.225">Lefton-Greif et al 2000</a>]. For this reason, affected individuals and their caregivers may tend to reduce their meals, potentially leading to insufficient caloric intake [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.rothblumoviatt.2016.159">Rothblum-Oviatt et al 2016</a>].</p><p>Oculomotor apraxia in individuals with classic A-T include gaze-evoked nystagmus, hypometric saccades, saccadic intrusions, convergence/accommodation, and vestibulo-ocular reflex abnormalities [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lewis.1999.287">Lewis et al 1999</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.tang.2019.556">Tang &#x00026; Shaikh 2019</a>]. These abnormalities progress over time.</p><p>As the result of the complex eye movement disorders, visual fixation can be difficult and can, for example, affect reading [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lewis.1999.287">Lewis et al 1999</a>].</p><p><b>Increased susceptibility to malignancy.</b> As result of the DNA repair defect that characterizes A-T, the risk of developing malignancies is increased (25%), with a median age at diagnosis of 12.5 years [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.suarez.2015.202">Suarez et al 2015</a>].</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Children</b> are prone to developing lymphomas (B&#x02010;cell non&#x02010;Hodgkin lymphoma and Hodgkin lymphoma) and leukemias (most notably T&#x02010;cell acute lymphoblastic leukemias and T&#x02010;cell prolymphocytic leukemia) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.taylor.1996.423">Taylor et al 1996</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bakhtiar.2021.879">Bakhtiar et al 2021</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Adults</b>. In addition to hematologic malignancies, adults are prone to developing solid tumors including breast cancer, ovarian cancer, gastric cancer, liver cancer, esophageal carcinomas, melanomas, leiomyomas, and sarcomas [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.rothblumoviatt.2016.159">Rothblum-Oviatt et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Abnormal cognition and behavior.</b> Only a limited number of studies have investigated cognition in individuals with A-T. Although it is generally accepted that intellectual disability is not a common finding in A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.vinck.2011.315">Vinck et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2014.297">Hoche et al 2014</a>], disturbances in cerebellar as well as non-cerebellar brain areas and networks may result in cognitive deficits [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2014.297">Hoche et al 2014</a>]. Noted deficits include problems with intellectual functioning, nonverbal memory, verbal abstract reasoning and calculation, and executive function [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.rothblumoviatt.2016.159">Rothblum-Oviatt et al 2016</a>] that overlap with the so-called cerebellar cognitive affective syndrome [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2019.225">Hoche et al 2019</a>]. The first manifestations start at the end of the first decade of life.</p><p><b>Immunodeficiency</b> is highly variable with abnormalities of humoral immunity, cellular immunity, or combined immune deficiency [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.driessen.2013.1367">Driessen et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.chopra.2014.275">Chopra et al 2014</a>]. In most individuals, the immunodeficiency remains stable over time [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.chopra.2014.275">Chopra et al 2014</a>].</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm"><b>Humoral immunity.</b> Impaired antigen receptor <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recombination/">recombination</a> and class-switch recombination result in a suboptimal production of a diverse repertoire of B cells [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.driessen.2013.1367">Driessen et al 2013</a>]. The most common deficiencies in humoral immunity are low levels of immunoglobulins, particularly selective IgA deficiency, hypogammaglobulinemia, and IgG subclass deficiency (most commonly involving IgG2 and IgG4) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>].</div><div class="half_rhythm">About 10% of individuals with IgA and IgG deficiency have normal or elevated levels of IgM, the so-called hyper IgM <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.noordzij.2009.448">Noordzij et al 2009</a>]. Due to severe immune deficiency, life span in individuals with hyper IgM is shorter than life span in individuals with classic A-T without hyper IgM [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al 2017b</a>]. Respiratory failure is a common cause of death in these individuals.</div><div class="half_rhythm">A reduced selective polysaccharide antibody response can also occur, mostly in individuals with deficiencies of both IgA and IgG2 [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.sanal.1999.326">Sanal et al 1999</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div class="half_rhythm"><b>Cellular immunity.</b> Thymic hypoplasia causes T-cell deficiency. The most common deficiency in cellular immunity is lymphopenia with reduced numbers of CD4<sup>+</sup> and CD8<sup>+</sup> cells [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.driessen.2013.1367">Driessen et al 2013</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Infection.</b> Despite the known immunodeficiencies in individuals with classic A-T, severe infections (bacterial, viral, and opportunistic) are uncommon [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>].</p><p>In contrast, mild sinopulmonary infections occur quite frequently and may aggravate pulmonary disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2010.847">McGrath-Morrow et al 2010</a>]. These recurrent upper respiratory tract infections occur at all ages, unlike lower respiratory infections, which are more common in older individuals [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nowakwegrzyn.2004.505">Nowak-Wegrzyn et al 2004</a>].</p><p>Viruses are the most common cause of respiratory tract infections during the first two years of life in both healthy children and children with A-T.</p><p>Bacterial causes of respiratory tract infections are <i>Haemophilus influenzae</i>, <i>Streptococcus pneumoniae</i>, and <i>Staphylococcus aureus</i> in children under age 15 years [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.davies.2009.565">Davies 2009</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bhatt.2014.1797">Bhatt &#x00026; Bush 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schroeder.2014.389">Schroeder &#x00026; Zielen 2014</a>]; <i>Pseudomonas aeruginosa</i> is more common in older individuals [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schroeder.2014.389">Schroeder &#x00026; Zielen 2014</a>].</p><p><b>Pulmonary disease,</b> present in a majority of individuals with A-T, causes significant morbidity and mortality. Pulmonary disease can be attributed to a combination of recurrent infections, immune deficiency, aspiration, interstitial lung disease, and neurologic abnormalities [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2000.225">Lefton-Greif et al 2000</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bott.2007.1021">Bott et al 2007</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2010.847">McGrath-Morrow et al 2010</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2021.1637">McGrath-Morrow et al 2021</a>].</p><p>Recurrent respiratory tract infections can result in bronchiectasis and pleural abnormalities [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schroeder.2014.389">Schroeder &#x00026; Zielen 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2021.1637">McGrath-Morrow et al 2021</a>].</p><p>Interstitial lung disease, which commonly manifests as dry cough, tachypnea, hypoxemia, and dyspnea (in the absence of viral or bacterial infections), usually occurs in adolescence.</p><p>Neuromuscular and central nervous system involvement can lead to dysphagia (commonly evident in the second decade) and chronic aspiration, aggravating existing lung disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2000.225">Lefton-Greif et al 2000</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2021.1637">McGrath-Morrow et al 2021</a>].</p><p>Pulmonary disease progresses with increasing age and neurologic involvement.</p><p><b>Endocrine abnormalities</b> include growth impairment, gonadal dysfunction, and insulin resistance [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.pommerening.2015.155">Pommerening et al 2015</a>].</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Poor linear growth,</b> due to underlying nutritional problems, recurrent infections, and suboptimal serum growth hormone levels, is common [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ehlayel.2014.s93">Ehlayel et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.voss.2014.123">Voss et al 2014</a>]. In addition, it is thought that growth failure is a primary A-T-related issue, being more prominent in females than males [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2016.889">Nissenkorn et al 2016</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>
<b>Abnormal puberty</b>
</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Males</b>. Although the levels of follicle-stimulation hormone (FSH), luteinizing hormone (LH), and testosterone are typically within the normal range, gonadal failure and abnormal spermatogenesis have also been described [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.sedgwick.1972">Sedgwick 1972</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2016.889">Nissenkorn et al 2016</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Females</b>. Elevated LH and FSH levels are common in adolescent females with A-T, indicating ovarian failure. When gonadotropin levels are elevated, clinical findings range from complete absence of pubertal development to normal sexual development and normal menstruation [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2016.889">Nissenkorn et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Insulin resistance,</b> present in some adults, typically manifests as hyperglycemia, hyperinsulinemia, and peripheral insulin resistance. Diabetes is rarely associated with glycosuria or ketosis [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schalch.1970.1396">Schalch et al 1970</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Other.</b> Thyroid and adrenal function are generally normal [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ammann.1970.587">Ammann et al 1970</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2016.889">Nissenkorn et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Telangiectasias,</b> vascular abnormalities occurring in the conjunctiva and sun-exposed skin (e.g., ears, face, neck, hands), are usually evident by age six years, but not at disease onset [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.greenberger.2013.932">Greenberger et al 2013</a>]. Overtime, telangiectasias can occur in other organs such as the bladder (described after treatment with intravenous cyclophosphamide) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.cohen.2008.667">Cohen et al 2008</a>], gastrointestinal tract, and brain [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.habek.2008.192">Habek et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schoenaker.2018.284">Schoenaker et al 2018</a>]. Telangiectasias in the brain usually need no therapeutic intervention. When telangiectasias occur in other organs, no standard therapeutic recommendations are available.</p><p>
<b>Other findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Premature aging</b> (resulting from the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genomic/">genomic</a> instability caused by the DNA repair defect that characterizes A-T) can manifest as: (1) graying hair and skin abnormalities, such as hypopigmentation and caf&#x000e9; au lait spots; and (2) adolescent onset of age-related diseases such as diabetes mellitus and liver disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.shiloh.2017.76">Shiloh &#x00026; Lederman 2017</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Radiation</b>
<b>sensitivity</b><b>.</b> Due to impairment of repair of DNA double-strand breaks, individuals with A-T have an increased sensitivity to ionizing radiation (x-ray and gamma ray) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lavin.1999.1201">Lavin &#x00026; Khanna 1999</a>] and radiomimetic drugs. Severe side effects from these treatments have been reported [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.perlman.2003">Perlman et al 2003</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Cutaneous granulomas,</b> described in some individuals [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.chiam.2011.13">Chiam et al 2011</a>], are thought to be related to vaccine-derived rubella virus [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bodemer.2014.o656">Bodemer et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.buchbinder.2019.81">Buchbinder et al 2019</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Elevated serum liver enzymes and hepatic steatosis</b> are often seen in adults [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2020b.830">van Os et al 2020b</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Intrafamilial variability.</b> Although family members with A-T may show some differences in clinical presentation and laboratory findings (including immunologic abnormalities), the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> within a family tends to be similar [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.taylor.2015.199">Taylor et al 2015</a>].</p><p><b>Life expectancy in classic A-T</b> is significantly reduced due to cancer, pulmonary disease, and infections. Most affected individuals do not live longer than age 30 years [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.crawford.2006.610">Crawford et al 2006</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.micol.2011">Micol et al 2011</a>].</p><p>Individuals with the hyper IgM <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> of classic A-T have a poorer prognosis than individuals with classic A-T, and most die before age 15 years due to respiratory failure [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al 2017b</a>].</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Variant_AT_1"><h4>Variant A-T</h4><p><b>Neurologic</b> manifestations vary.</p><p>The first manifestations of variant A-T can occur in childhood to adulthood. Most individuals have their first manifestations by age ten years.</p><p>Cerebellar ataxia can be absent in variant A-T, in contrast to classic A-T.</p><p>Extrapyramidal movement disorders are common, the most predominant of which are dystonia and dystonic tremor. Most individuals who have a purely extrapyramidal presentation tend to have a milder disease course [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019b.e19">van Os et al 2019b</a>].</p><p>Chorea and parkinsonism are rare in variant A-T.</p><p>Most individuals with variant A-T have, in addition to central motor manifestations, signs and symptoms of an axonal sensorimotor polyneuropathy [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p>Rarely, individuals with variant A-T have anterior horn cell disease, which can either be one of the presenting features or can manifest during the disease course [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019b.e19">van Os et al 2019b</a>].</p><p><b>Oculomotor apraxia.</b> Although abnormal eye movements can include nystagmus, jerky ocular pursuit, and hypo- and hypermetric saccades, individuals with variant A-T can have normal eye movements [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Feeding/nutrition.</b> Occasionally, nutritional problems can occur in individuals with variant A-T due to neurologic decline [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Increased susceptibility to malignancy.</b> Individuals with variant A-T have an increased risk of developing malignancies (29.5%) [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019b.e19">van Os et al 2019b</a>]. In particular, premenopausal females are at increased risk to develop breast cancer and hematologic malignancies [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Abnormal cognition and behavior.</b> No information is available on these findings in variant A-T.</p><p><b>Immunodeficiency.</b> Serum immunoglobulin levels and T- and B-cell counts are generally normal [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009a.430">Verhagen et al 2009a</a>].</p><p><b>Infection.</b> Respiratory infections do not occur at a frequency greater than that in the general population [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009a.430">Verhagen et al 2009a</a>].</p><p><b>Pulmonary disease</b> is not a major feature in variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Endocrine abnormalities.</b> Th prevalence of endocrine abnormalities like diabetes mellitus, growth impairment, and gonadal dysfunction in individuals with variant A-T seems to be similar to that in the general population [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009a.430">Verhagen et al 2009a</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Telangiectasias</b> have been reported in about 55% of individuals with variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019b.e19">van Os et al 2019b</a>].</p><p>
<b>Other findings</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Premature aging,</b> from a clinical perspective, is not a clearly recognizable feature of variant A-T; however, no studies have investigated the lifelong risks of common age-related disorders such diabetes mellitus, cardiovascular disease, and neurodegenerative disorders like Parkinson disease.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Radiation</b>
<b>sensitivity</b><b>.</b> Individuals with variant A-T have an increased sensitivity to ionizing radiation (x-ray and gamma ray) and radiomimetic drugs [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009a.430">Verhagen et al 2009a</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Intrafamilial variability.</b> In a study of 57 individuals with variant A-T that included five sets of sibs, the neurologic phenotypes of sibs were similar [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Life expectancy in variant A-T.</b> The longer life expectancy in variant A-T than in classic A-T can be attributed to less progressive neurologic decline, typical absence of respiratory and immunologic features, and older age of occurrence of malignancies [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al 2017b</a>].</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes"><h4>Heterozygotes</h4><p>Although individuals <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a pathogenic <i>ATM</i> variant are not at risk for A-T, their risk of developing cancer is increased compared to that of the general population. In particular, heterozygous females younger than age 50 years have an increased risk of developing breast cancer [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.olsen.2005.260">Olsen et al 2005</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>].</p><p>In addition to this increased cancer risk, heterozygotes may also have an increased risk of developing cardiovascular disease, diabetes mellitus, and neurodegenerative disorders; however, systematic population studies to (quantitatively) investigate this issue are lacking [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>].</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.GenotypePhenotype_Co"><h3>Genotype-Phenotype Correlations</h3><p>In general, <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/nonsense-variant/">nonsense</a> and/or frameshift variants lead to the classic A-T <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>, whereas <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a> and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/splice-site/">splice site</a> variants are more typically associated with variant A-T. Within families, phenotypes of affected individuals are generally similar [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2012.561">Verhagen et al 2012</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.taylor.2015.199">Taylor et al 2015</a>].</p><p><b>Specific <i>ATM</i> variants</b> with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genotype-phenotype-correlations/">genotype-phenotype correlations</a> include the following (see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar">Table 6</a>):</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.3576G&#x0003e;A.</b></a> Individuals who are either <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> or <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a> for this <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/splice-site/">splice site</a> variant have milder classic A-T with prolonged survival and lower susceptibility to immunodeficiency, respiratory disease, and cancer [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2019a.308">van Os et al 2019a</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.5763-1050A&#x0003e;G.</b></a> This <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/splice-site/">splice site</a> variant, associated with the A-T variant <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>, is a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/founder-variant/">founder variant</a> in the United Kingdom [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcconville.1996.320">McConville et al 1996</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.stewart.2001.30133">Stewart et al 2001</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Compound heterozygosity for <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.6154G&#x0003e;A</b></a> (a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a> variant) and <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.7886_7890delTATTA</b></a> (a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/frameshift-variant/">frameshift variant</a>) result in the variant A-T <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> with dopa-responsive dystonia [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.charlesworth.2013.1148">Charlesworth et al 2013</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.6200C&#x0003e;A.</b></a> This <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a> variant in Canadian Mennonites is associated with a dystonia-predominant variant A-T <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.saunderspullman.2012.649">Saunders-Pullman et al 2012</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.7271T&#x0003e;G.</b></a> Four of six individuals <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> for this <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a> variant had variant A-T and normal serum alpha-fetoprotein levels [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>]. This variant is associated with a high risk of breast cancer [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.stankovic.1998.334">Stankovic et al 1998</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar"><b>c.8147T&#x0003e;C.</b></a> All individuals who are <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/compound-heterozygous/">compound heterozygous</a> for this <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/missense/">missense</a> variant have variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2020b.830">van Os et al 2020b</a>].</div></li></ul></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Nomenclature"><h3>Nomenclature</h3><p>Ataxia-telangiectasia was previously referred to as Louis-Bar syndrome.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Prevalence"><h3>Prevalence</h3><p>The reported incidence of A-T, a rare disorder, varies between 1:300,000 and 1:40,000. The estimated prevalence is 1-9:100,000 [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.swift.1986.573">Swift et al 1986</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.rothblumoviatt.2016.159">Rothblum-Oviatt et al 2016</a>].</p><p>In the United States approximately 350 children with A-T are known to the patient organization A-T Children's Project (see <a href="https://www.atcp.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">www.atcp.org</a>).</p><p>Based on the incidence of A-T in the United States, the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a> frequency is estimated to be one in 200 persons [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.swift.1986.573">Swift et al 1986</a>].</p><p>Increased <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a> frequencies have been reported in the following populations due to founder variants (see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar">Table 6</a>):</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>One in three to one in 15 in the Druze population in northern Israel [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.fares.2004.358">Fares et al 2004</a>]</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>One in 81 in the Moroccan and Tunisian Jewish population [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.gilad.1996.2033">Gilad et al 1996</a>]</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>One in 36 in the Romani population in Spain [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mancebo.2007.861">Mancebo et al 2007</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.carranza.2017.161">Carranza et al 2017</a>]</div></li></ul></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Genetically_Related"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Genetically_Related_">Genetically Related (Allelic) Disorders</h2><p>No phenotypes other than those discussed in this <i>GeneReview</i> are known to be associated with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a> pathogenic variants in <i>ATM</i>.</p><p><b>Sporadic tumors</b> occurring as single tumors in the absence of any other findings of ataxia-telangiectasia frequently contain a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/somatic-pathogenic-variant/">somatic pathogenic variant</a> in <i>ATM</i> that is <b>not</b> present in the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/germline/">germline</a>. In these circumstances predisposition to these tumors is not heritable.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Differential_Diagnos"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Differential_Diagnos_">Differential Diagnosis</h2><p>Genetic disorders associated with eye findings and ataxia in the differential diagnosis of ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T) are listed in <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas3">Table 3</a>. Notably, none of these disorders is associated with immunologic abnormalities. For review of the differential diagnosis of cerebellar ataxia, see <a href="/books/n/gene/ataxias/">Hereditary Ataxia Overview</a>.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 3. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Differential Diagnosis</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Gene</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Disorder&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup></th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Onset (in yrs)</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Additional Features of This Disorder Not Observed in A-T</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>APTX</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ataxia w/oculomotor apraxia type 1 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/208920" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">208920</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">2-10</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Hypoalbuminemia</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>&#x02191; serum total cholesterol</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Generally normal serum AFP</div></li></ul>
