</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>FXN</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/books/n/gene/friedreich/">Friedreich ataxia</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">10-15</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Pyramidal &#x00026; sensory signs</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Optic atrophy</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Cardiomyopathy</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Normal serum AFP</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PIK3R5</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ataxia w/oculomotor apraxia type 3 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/615217" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">615217</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">12-18</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"></td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>PNKP</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ataxia w/oculomotor apraxia type 4 (OMIM <a href="https://omim.org/entry/616267" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">616267</a>)</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">&#x0003c;10</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Hypoalbuminemia</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>&#x02191; cholesterol</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>SETX</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/books/n/gene/aoa2/">Ataxia w/oculomotor apraxia type 2</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">3-30</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Pyramidal signs</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<i>TTPA</i>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<a href="/books/n/gene/aved/">Ataxia w/vitamin E deficiency</a>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">5-15</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_3_1_1_1_4" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Low plasma levels of vitamin E</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Pyramidal signs</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>&#x02193; visual acuity</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Normal serum AFP</div></li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">Based on <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.a">van Os et al [2020a]</a></p></div></dd><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">AFP = alpha-fetoprotein; A-T = ataxia-telangiectasia</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.3.1"><p class="no_margin">The disorders listed in this table are inherited in an <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner.</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Management"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Management_">Management</h2><p>Most of the guidelines recommended for the management of health-related problems in individuals with ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T) are expert and evidence based, due to a lack of clinical trials; see <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.rothblumoviatt.2016.159">Rothblum-Oviatt et al [2016]</a> (<a href="https://ojrd.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s13023-016-0543-7" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>), <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schoenaker.2016.641">Schoenaker et al [2016]</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al [2017b]</a> (<a href="https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/dmcn.13424" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>), <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.amirifar.2020.859">Amirifar et al [2020]</a>, and <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2021.1637">McGrath-Morrow et al [2021]</a> (<a href="https://www.dovepress.com/multidisciplinary-management-of-ataxia-telangiectasia-current-perspect-peer-reviewed-fulltext-article-JMDH" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>).</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Evaluations_Followin"><h3>Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</h3><p>To establish the extent of disease and needs in an individual diagnosed with ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T), the evaluations summarized in <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas4">Table 4</a> (if not performed as part of the evaluation that led to the diagnosis) are recommended.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 4. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Recommended Evaluations Following Initial Diagnosis</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" rowspan="2" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="2" scope="col" colspan="1" headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3" colspan="2" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:center;vertical-align:middle;">Comment</th></tr><tr><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" colspan="1" scope="colgroup" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Classic A-T</th><th headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3" id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Variant A-T</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By neurologist familiar w/A-T, when possible</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess for ataxia (w/SARA&#x000a0;<sup>1</sup> &#x00026;/or ICARS) &#x00026; extrapyramidal movement disorders such as dystonia, chorea, parkinsonism, myoclonus &#x00026; tremor. Consider using specific scales for A-T such as A-T NEST&#x000a0;<sup>2</sup> or ATFS</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Rehabilitation</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Orthopedics&#x000a0;/ physical medicine &#x00026; rehab&#x000a0;/ PT &#x00026; OT eval</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To incl assessment of:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Gross motor &#x00026; fine motor skills</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Contractures &#x00026; scoliosis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Mobility, ADL, &#x00026; need for adaptive devices</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Need for PT (to improve gross motor skills) &#x00026;/or OT (to improve fine motor skills)</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dysarthria</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By speech-language pathologist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluate speech production &#x00026; language.</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dysphagia/Feeding/</b>
<br />
<b>Nutrition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Nutrition&#x000a0;/ feeding team eval</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To incl eval of aspiration risk &#x00026; nutritional status</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Consider eval for gastrostomy tube placement if nutritional status is poor &#x00026;/or if there is dysphagia &#x00026;/or &#x02191; risk of aspiration.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern early in disease course</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Oculomotor problems</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Exam by neurologist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Specific eval by ophthalmologist or eye specialist not regularly required; only if indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Cognition/Behavior</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By neurologist or OT familiar w/A-T, when possible</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern. Because (moderate to) severe ID is not a hallmark of A-T, if there are such concerns, an additional cause should be sought.</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Increased susceptibility to malignancy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment by doctor of internal medicine&#x000a0;/ pediatrician</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">In all persons:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval for clinical manifestations of malignancy (e.g., lymphadenopathy)</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Laboratory tests to assess for hematologic malignancies (per annual screening; see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas5">Table 5</a>)</div></li></ul>
In adults: breast MRI (in females) &#x00026; abdominal echo (per annual screening; see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas5">Table 5</a>)</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Immunodeficiency</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment by immunologist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluate:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>For humoral &#x00026; cellular immune defects;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Whether immunoglobulin substitution therapy is indicated;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Vaccination status.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Infection</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment by primary care clinician&#x000a0;/ pulmonologist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess for sinopulmonary infection.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Determine need for prophylactic antibiotic treatment.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pulmonary disease</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment by immunologist&#x000a0;/ pulmonologist&#x000a0;/ doctor of internal medicine&#x000a0;/ pediatrician</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess for pulmonary function&#x000a0;<sup>3</sup> &#x00026; common causes of pulmonary disease.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess lung function when possible (often age &#x0003e;4 yrs).</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine abnormalities</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assessment by doctor of internal medicine&#x000a0;/ pediatrician</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess length/height in children (using standard growth charts).</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Assess age-appropriate pubertal development.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Screening for diabetes, cardiovascular disease, &#x00026; hepatic disease in adolescents &#x00026; adults</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Usually not a concern</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Genetic counseling</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By genetics professionals&#x000a0;<sup>4</sup></td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">To obtain a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pedigree/">pedigree</a> &#x00026; inform affected persons &#x00026; their families re nature, MOI, &#x00026; implications of A-T (&#x00026; heterozygosity for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>) to facilitate medical &#x00026; personal decision making</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family support</b>
<br />
<b>&#x00026; resources</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess need for:
<ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Community or <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Resources">online resources</a> such as <a href="https://www.p2pusa.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Parent to Parent</a>;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Social work involvement for parental support;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Palliative care involvement &#x00026;/or home nursing referral.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_1_3 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_1 hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_4_1_1_2_2" colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">For difficult life-prolonging decisions or for clarification of treatment options, consider further consultation w/independent clinical teams.&#x000a0;<sup>5</sup></td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">ADL = activities of daily living; ATFS = Ataxia Telangiectasia Functioning Scale; A-T NEST = Ataxia-Telangiectasia Neurological Examination Scale Toolkit; ICARS = International Cooperative Ataxia Rating Scale; ID = intellectual disability; ILD = interstitial lung disease; MOI = <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/mode-of-inheritance/">mode of inheritance</a>; OT = occupational therapy; PT = physical therapy; SARA = Scale for the Assessment and Rating of Ataxia</p></div></dd><dt>1. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.4.1"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schmitzh_bsch.2006.1717">Schmitz-H&#x000fc;bsch et al [2006]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>2. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.4.2"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.jackson.2016.690">Jackson et al [2016]</a>
</p></div></dd><dt>3. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.4.3"><p class="no_margin">Regarding interstitial lung disease (ILD), spirometry can detect restrictive lung disease. Decreased forced vital capacity (FVC) is possibly the result of abnormalities in respiratory muscle coordination or scoliosis. Helium dilution measurements can help discriminate between true restrictive lung disease and an inability to expire to residual volume. Although a lung biopsy can help confirm the diagnosis of ILD, the diagnostic benefits should be weighed against the risks of the procedure [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2010.847">McGrath-Morrow et al 2010</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2021.1637">McGrath-Morrow et al 2021</a>].</p></div></dd><dt>4. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.4.4"><p class="no_margin">Clinical geneticist, certified genetic counselor, certified genetic nurse, genetics advanced practice provider (nurse practitioner or physician assistant)</p></div></dd><dt>5. </dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TF.4.5"><p class="no_margin">
<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.linney.2019.413">Linney et al [2019]</a>
</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Treatment_of_Manifes"><h3>Treatment of Manifestations</h3><p>There is no cure for ataxia-telangiectasia.</p><p><b>Supportive care</b> to improve quality of life, maximize function, and reduce complications is recommended. This ideally involves multidisciplinary care by specialists in (pediatric) neurology, pulmonology, immunology, pediatrics (for children) and internal medicine (for adults), rehabilitation medicine, and professionals in physical therapy, speech-language therapy, occupational therapy, and nutrition. For specific issues care may be provided by specialists in oncology, medical genetics, endocrinology, orthopedics, dermatology, mental health, and social work [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017a.680">van Os et al 2017a</a>] (<a href="https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/epdf/10.1111/dmcn.13424" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>).</p><p>The following discussion takes into consideration that distinctions between the findings in classic A-T and variant A-T are not always possible; thus, clinicians need to focus on the issues that are most relevant to each affected individual.</p><p><b>Ataxia.</b> Nicotinamide riboside, a form of vitamin B<sub>3</sub>, at 25 mg/kg/day (maximum dose of 900 mg/day), has significantly reduced ataxia scores (i.e., improved the motor disorder) in a single trial with 24 individuals with both classic and variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021a.2951">Veenhuis et al 2021a</a>].</p><p><b>Extrapyramidal movement disorders.</b> The effectiveness of pharmacotherapy in movement disorders in A-T is low to moderate. Only a few non-randomized studies have investigated the treatment of movement disorders in individuals with A-T specifically [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.zannolli.2012.1312">Zannolli et al 2012</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2013.155">Nissenkorn et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.shaikh.2013.2728">Shaikh et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leuzzi.2015.e98">Leuzzi et al 2015</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021a.2951">Veenhuis et al 2021a</a>].</p><p><b>Dystonia,</b> the most troublesome movement disorder in A-T, can vary in distribution and severity and varies among affected individuals.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Focal dystonia.</b> As in individuals with torticollis, botulinum toxin A is the treatment of first choice.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Generalized dystonia.</b> Anticholinergic drugs and GABA mimetics can be helpful.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Generalized, dopa-responsive dystonia.</b> A trial with levodopa may be useful in some individuals [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.charlesworth.2013.1148">Charlesworth et al 2013</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Deep brain stimulation (DBS)</b> of the globus pallidus pars interna, described to date in two individuals with A-T and dystonia, had conflicting results [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.georgiev.2016.405">Georgiev et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul><p>For recommendations on the treatment of ataxia, chorea, myoclonus, and tremor, see <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017b.45">van Os et al [2017b]</a>.</p><p><b>Rehabilitation&#x000a0;/ activities of daily living.</b> The main goal of supportive treatment by a rehabilitation specialist (physiatrist), physical therapists, occupational therapists, and allied health care workers is the maintenance of physical function and condition and prevention of complications such as contractures. A multidisciplinary approach is the cornerstone of the treatment of motor disability.</p><p>Physical therapists can provide exercises to maintain muscle strength, overall condition, and activity and to prevent joint contractures. Necessary aids can include:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Foot orthoses;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A (wheel)chair to provide a good upright sitting position to reduce scoliosis and prevent choking;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Standing frames.</div></li></ul><p>Occupational therapists can provide aids and devices for activities of daily living.</p><p><b>Dysarthria.</b> A speech-language therapist can help address dysarthria and provide practical advice to improve speech intelligibility and support families [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021b.450">Veenhuis et al 2021b</a>]. In most individuals with A-T, oral communication is possible. When dysarthria is severe, a speech-language therapist can suggest use of communication aids (e.g., <a href="https://www.asha.org/NJC/AAC/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">augmentative and alternative communication</a> [AAC]) and equipment.</p><p><b>Dysphagia/feeding/nutrition.</b> Management of dysphagia may involve feeding teams that include speech-language therapists, nutritionists (regarding thickened liquids and caloric intake), and gastroenterologists (who recommend gastrostomy feeding when appropriate).</p><p>Good nutritional status is required for optimal effectiveness of all other treatments in A-T. Individuals with A-T are often malnourished, as a result of the combination of physical difficulties with eating (i.e., oral-motor problems affecting chewing and swallowing), easy fatigability, and endocrine abnormalities that affect growth [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2000.225">Lefton-Greif et al 2000</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2011.23">Lefton-Greif et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ehlayel.2014.s93">Ehlayel et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ross.2015.802">Ross et al 2015</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.nissenkorn.2016.889">Nissenkorn et al 2016</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.natale.2021.1">Natale et al 2021</a>]. In addition to these secondary factors, the underlying molecular defect itself seems to make affected individuals prone to have poor linear growth.</p><p>Practical tips such as attention to a good sitting position, use of a straw, thickening of thin liquids, and giving children easily chewable foods may help [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ross.2015.802">Ross et al 2015</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017a.680">van Os et al 2017a</a>].</p><p>Gastrostomy placement early in the disease course can improve clinical outcomes [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.leftongreif.2011.23">Lefton-Greif et al 2011</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ross.2015.802">Ross et al 2015</a>].</p><p><b>Oculomotor problems.</b> Ophthalmologists can advise regarding visual aids that may be helpful (e.g., for reading) in the context of eye movement abnormalities.</p><p>Nystagmus and oculomotor apraxia generally do not require drug treatment.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>4-aminopyridine did improve oculomotor and vestibular function in a small case series [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.shaikh.2013.2728">Shaikh et al 2013</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Acetyl-DL-leucine was shown to have an effect on downbeat nystagmus in a small study [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.brueggemann.2022.20">Brueggemann et al 2022</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Malignancy.</b> To date, there are no specific consensus treatment protocols for malignancies in A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.machida.2013.482">Machida et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.p_rezvillena.2013.308">P&#x000e9;rez-Villena et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schoenaker.2016.641">Schoenaker et al 2016</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017a.680">van Os et al 2017a</a>].</p><p>Treatment protocols for an individual with A-T with a malignancy should be individualized based on patient-related factors (such as mobility, lung function) and malignancy-related factors (such as type of malignancy and alternative treatment options).</p><p><b>Chemotherapy</b> in general can be initiated with dose-adjusted protocols; doses can be increased when chemotherapy is tolerated.</p><p><b>Radiation therapy</b> is contraindicated in individuals with classic A-T and variant A-T because increased radiosensitivity may lead to very severe complications.</p><p><b>Allogenic stem cell transplantation,</b> described in several individuals with A-T and a malignancy, has had differing outcomes. There seems to be a serious risk of adverse events when this therapy is given without an adjusted conditioning regime [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ghosh.2012.438">Ghosh et al 2012</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ussowicz.2013.740">Ussowicz et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.beier.2016.1271">Beier et al 2016</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bakhtiar.2018.2495">Bakhtiar et al 2018</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.slack.2018">Slack et al 2018</a>].</p><p><b>Cognition.</b> When there are concerns about cognition, the following information represents typical management recommendations for individuals with developmental delay&#x000a0;/ intellectual disability in the United States; standard recommendations may vary from country to country.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Ages 0-3 years.</b> Referral to an early intervention program is recommended for access to occupational, physical, speech, and feeding therapy as well as infant mental health services, special educators, and sensory impairment specialists. In the US, early intervention is a federally funded program available in all states that provides in-home services to target individual therapy needs.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>Ages 3-5 years.</b> In the US, developmental preschool through the local public school district is recommended. Before placement, an evaluation is made to determine needed services and therapies and an individualized education plan (IEP) is developed for those who qualify based on established motor, language, social, or cognitive delay. The early intervention program typically assists with this transition. Developmental preschool is center based; for children too medically unstable to attend, home-based services are provided.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><b>All ages.</b> Consultation with a developmental pediatrician is recommended to ensure the involvement of appropriate community, state, and educational agencies (US) and to support parents in maximizing quality of life. Some issues to consider:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>An IEP provides specially designed instruction and related services to children who qualify.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A 504 plan (Section 504: a US federal statute that prohibits discrimination based on disability) can be considered for those who require accommodations or modifications such as front-of-class seating, assistive technology devices, classroom scribes, extra time between classes, modified assignments, and enlarged text.</div></li></ul></li></ul><p><b>Behavior.</b> Over time individuals with A-T are prone to develop cerebellar cognitive affective syndrome [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2014.297">Hoche et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hoche.2019.225">Hoche et al 2019</a>], for which monitoring is warranted (see <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Surveillance">Surveillance</a>).</p><p>As individuals with A-T have a severe disorder, attention should be paid to their emotional well-being [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2020b.830">van Os et al 2020b</a>].</p><p><b>Prophylactic antibiotic treatment</b> should be considered early in the disease course in individuals with recurrent or severe infections and in those with immunoglobulin G deficiency or specific polysaccharide antibody deficiency [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2017a.680">van Os et al 2017a</a>].</p><p><b>Immunoglobulin replacement therapy</b> is indicated in individuals with severe IgG deficiency and in those with the hyper IgM <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a>. It may be considered in individuals with recurrent infections, mild humoral immune defects, or low antibody responses despite booster immunizations [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.davies.2009.565">Davies 2009</a>].</p><p><b>Immunizations.</b> Most children have had their immunizations before the diagnosis of A-T is established; generally, this goes without any complications. Inactivated vaccines are safe in all persons with A-T. Flu vaccines, 13-valent pneumococcal conjugate, and 23-valent pneumococcal polysaccharide vaccines should be given to all individuals with A-T.</p><p>Of note, in individuals with severe immunodeficiency, rubella vaccination should be avoided, as it can possibly increase the risk for granulomas [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bodemer.2014.o656">Bodemer et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.buchbinder.2019.81">Buchbinder et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Respiratory.</b> The European Respiratory Society (ERS) has prepared extensive guidelines for the multidisciplinary respiratory management of A-T, emphasizing the need for monitoring of immune function, recurrent infection, pulmonary function, swallowing, nutrition, and scoliosis, all of which could contribute to increased respiratory morbidity and mortality in A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bhatt.2015.565">Bhatt et al 2015</a>] (<a href="https://err.ersjournals.com/content/24/138/565" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">full text</a>).</p><p><b>Anesthetic and perioperative risk.</b> Due to pulmonary problems and often poor nutritional status, individuals with A-T are at increased risk of developing complications during and shortly after anesthesia [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrath_morrow.2008.59">McGrath&#x02010;Morrow et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009b.101">Verhagen et al 2009b</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.mcgrathmorrow.2010.847">McGrath&#x02010;Morrow et al 2010</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lockman.2012.256">Lockman et al 2012</a>]. Therefore, individuals with A-T should be operated on in a hospital with specialists with expertise in A-T. Forced weaning and early extubation can improve outcomes, specifically in individuals with severe restrictive pulmonary dysfunction [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.verhagen.2009b.101">Verhagen et al 2009b</a>].</p><p><b>Endocrinology.</b> Young adults with A-T may develop insulin-resistant diabetes mellitus and hypercholesterolemia [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.andrade.2015.196">Andrade et al 2015</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.donath.2020.317">Donath et al 2020</a>], which can be treated following protocols for the general population. However, the shortened life expectancy of individuals with classic A-T can result in the decision not to treat high cholesterol levels, since treatment will not have any expected clinical consequences.</p><p>In females with gonadal failure (in case of primary or secondary amenorrhea), estrogen supplementation can prevent osteoporosis [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ehlayel.2014.s93">Ehlayel et al 2014</a>].</p><p>Growth hormone treatment may be an option for children with severe growth failure [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.woelke.2017.125">Woelke et al 2017</a>].</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Surveillance"><h3>Surveillance</h3><p>To monitor existing manifestations, the individual's response to supportive care, and the emergence of new manifestations, the evaluations summarized in <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTataxiatelangiectas5">Table 5</a> are recommended.</p><p>Note the recommended surveillance is the same for individuals with classic A-T and variant A-T, with the exception that individuals who do not have evidence of lung disease at the time of the initial diagnosis do not require annual screening for pulmonary disease.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5" class="table"><h3><span class="label">Table 5. </span></h3><div class="caption"><p>Ataxia-Telangiectasia: Recommended Surveillance</p></div><p class="large-table-link" style="display:none"><span class="right"><a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5/?report=objectonly" target="object">View in own window</a></span></p><div class="large_tbl" id="__ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_lrgtbl__"><table class="no_bottom_margin"><thead><tr><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">System/Concern</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Evaluation</th><th id="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" scope="col" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Frequency/Timing</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Neurologic</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Eval by neurologist for response to therapy for existing findings &#x00026; development of new findings</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If child has severe/profound ID, seek additional cause.</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Educational needs</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Screening for cognitive functioning &#x00026; any speech-language issues</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Before becoming school age; once school age, annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Behavior</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Monitoring for social-emotional development</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">When starting kindergarten &#x00026; again when entering secondary school</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>ADL/Musculoskeletal</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By PT, OT, &#x00026;/or rehab specialist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Per rehab team</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Dysarthria/Communication</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By SLP</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Per treating SLP</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Oculomotor problems</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By ophthalmologist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Only if indicated</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Immunodeficiency</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By immunologist (immunoglobulin levels, white blood cell count)</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="2" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:top;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Infection</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By immunologist</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Pulmonary disease</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>By pulmonologist</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Lung function testing (by spirometry)</div></li></ul>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually for persons w/evidence of disease at time of initial diagnosis</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" rowspan="4" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Increased susceptibility to malignancy</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Clinical assessment for signs/symptoms of leukemia &#x00026;/or lymphoma incl for lymphadenopathy &#x00026; unexplained fever</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Blood count &#x00026; smear, immunoglobulin levels, M protein, LDH, IgM</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Immediate referral to oncologist if malignancy is diagnosed or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Ultrasound exam of abdomen in adulthood</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Annually</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Breast MRI in females</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually starting at age 25 yrs</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" rowspan="3" scope="rowgroup" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Endocrine abnormalities</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess length/height in children.</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually in childhood</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Assess age-appropriate pubertal development.</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" colspan="1" scope="row" rowspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">Diabetes screening: urinalysis, fasting blood glucose concentration, Hgb A1c</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually in adults</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Feeding/Nutrition</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">By SLP/nutritionist</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;"><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Annually</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>More frequently if problems are present or suspected</div></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_1" scope="row" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">
<b>Family support</b>
<br />
<b>&#x00026; resources</b>
</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_2" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">All health care providers should pay attention to the well-being of persons w/A-T &#x00026; their families.</td><td headers="hd_h_ataxia-telangiectas.T.ataxiatelangiectas_5_1_1_1_3" rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="text-align:left;vertical-align:middle;">At each visit</td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div><p class="no_margin">Hgb = hemoglobin; ID = intellectual disability; LDH = lactate dehydrogenase; SLP = speech-language pathologist</p></div></dd></dl></div></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1"><h4>Heterozygotes</h4><p><b>Females</b> who are suspected heterozygotes for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> (such as mothers of children with A-T) should have <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> to confirm the presence of an <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variant and determine which <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variant is present in the mother to clarify her risk of breast cancer.</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Females <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for the <i>ATM</i> variant <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar">c.7271T&#x0003e;G</a> have a breast cancer risk that is similar to that of females with a <i>BRCA1</i> or <i>BRCA2</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>; thus, more intensive breast screening is recommended from age 25 years (see <i>BRCA1</i>- and <i>BRCA2</i>-Associated Hereditary Breast and Ovarian Cancer, <a href="/books/n/gene/brca1/#brca1.Surveillance">Surveillance</a>). To date, there is limited data on medical or surgical prophylactic treatments to decrease the risk of breast cancer in <i>ATM</i> heterozygotes [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.cragun.2020.421">Cragun et al 2020</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Females <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for any other <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> should have an annual mammogram beginning at age 40 years [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>]. Breast MRI can be considered based on additional factors including family history, age, and breast density.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Radiation therapy at conventional doses is not contraindicated in females <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Males and females</b> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for a pathogenic <i>ATM</i> variant are at increased risk of developing other types of tumors such as malignancies of the gastrointestinal tract as well as increased risk of cardiovascular disease [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>].</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To date, intensified screening for other types of cancer have not been recommended.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>To date, it is not recommended that heterozygotes avoid radiation therapy at conventional doses or diagnostic radiographs or CT scans.</div></li></ul><p>Heterozygotes should be made aware of lifestyle factors that contribute to cardiovascular diseases, but no specific screening has been recommended [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>].</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.AgentsCircumstances"><h3>Agents/Circumstances to Avoid</h3><p>
<b>Affected individuals</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Rubella vaccination should be avoided in individuals with severe immunodeficiency as it can possibly increase the risk for granulomas [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bodemer.2014.o656">Bodemer et al 2014</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.buchbinder.2019.81">Buchbinder et al 2019</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Ionizing radiation (x-ray and gamma ray) is contraindicated, due to increased sensitivity [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.lavin.1999.1201">Lavin &#x00026; Khanna 1999</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Radiation therapy is contraindicated because increased radiosensitivity may lead to very severe complications [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.perlman.2003">Perlman et al 2003</a>].</div></li></ul><p><b>Heterozygotes.</b> There are no agents/circumstances to avoid.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Evaluation_of_Relati"><h3>Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</h3><p>It is appropriate to offer <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/molecular-genetic-testing/">molecular genetic testing</a> for the <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants identified in the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> to apparently asymptomatic older and younger at-risk relatives of an affected individual in order to identify as early as possible:</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sibs with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants who would benefit from prompt initiation of treatment of manifestations of A-T, surveillance for malignancy, and awareness of <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.AgentsCircumstances">agents/circumstances to avoid</a>;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Family members who are <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and who would benefit from age-appropriate intensified surveillance for breast cancer (see Clinical Description, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes">Heterozygotes</a> and Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</div></li></ul><p>See <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Related_Genetic_Coun">Genetic Counseling</a> for issues related to testing of at-risk relatives for <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> purposes.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Therapies_Under_Inve"><h3>Therapies Under Investigation</h3><p>Currently, a multicenter randomized controlled trial with <b>intra-erythrocyte dexamethasone</b> in patients with A-T is being conducted (see <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT03563053" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NCT03563053</a>).</p><p><b>Nicotinamide riboside</b> (a form of vitamin B<sub>3</sub>) was shown to have a positive effect on ataxia scores and immunoglobulin levels in a proof-of-concept study [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.veenhuis.2021a.2951">Veenhuis et al 2021a</a>]. A second study on the same topic is being conducted (see <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/ct2/show/NCT04870866" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">NCT04870866</a>).</p><p><b>Gene therapy</b> with antisense oligonucleotides in A-T is currently being studied in a single case study in the United States (see <a href="https://www.atcp.org/a-t-aso-gene-therapy-trial-continues/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">www.atcp.org</a>). To date, no toxic side effects have been reported, but the clinical effects are not available yet.</p><p><b>Allogenic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation</b> in A-T has been conducted in single cases and small case series, with divergent results regarding effect on neurologic functioning [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.broccoletti.2008.223">Broccoletti et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ghosh.2012.438">Ghosh et al 2012</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.ussowicz.2013.740">Ussowicz et al 2013</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.beier.2016.1271">Beier et al 2016</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.bakhtiar.2018.2495">Bakhtiar et al 2018</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.slack.2018">Slack et al 2018</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.duecker.2019.2785">Duecker et al 2019</a>].</p><p><b>Treatment studies with steroids in individuals with A-T.</b> Although such studies have shown promising results for neurologic manifestations [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.buoni.2006.1479">Buoni et al 2006</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.broccoletti.2008.223">Broccoletti et al 2008</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.zannolli.2012.1312">Zannolli et al 2012</a>, <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.hasegawa.2019.60">Hasegawa et al 2019</a>], long-term use of steroids may potentially cause severe side effects.</p><p>Search <a href="https://clinicaltrials.gov/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ClinicalTrials.gov</a> in the US and <a href="https://www.clinicaltrialsregister.eu/ctr-search/search" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">EU Clinical Trials Register</a> in Europe for access to information on clinical studies for a wide range of diseases and conditions.</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Genetic_Counseling"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Genetic_Counseling_">Genetic Counseling</h2><p><i>Genetic counseling is the process of providing individuals and families with information on the nature, mode(s) of inheritance, and implications of genetic disorders to help them make informed medical and personal decisions. The following section deals with genetic risk assessment and the use of family history and genetic testing to clarify genetic status for family members; it is not meant to address all personal, cultural, or ethical issues that may arise or to substitute for consultation with a genetics professional</i>. &#x02014;ED.</p><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Mode_of_Inheritance"><h3>Mode of Inheritance</h3><p>Ataxia-telangiectasia (A-T) is caused by <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants in <i>ATM</i> and inherited an <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/autosomal-recessive/">autosomal recessive</a> manner.</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Risk_to_Family_Membe"><h3>Risk to Family Members</h3><p>
<b>Parents of a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The parents of an affected child are presumed to be <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Molecular genetic testing is recommended for the parents of a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> to confirm that both parents are <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and to allow reliable <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/recurrence-risk/">recurrence risk</a> assessment.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> is detected in only one parent and parental identity testing has confirmed biological maternity and paternity, it is possible that one of the pathogenic variants identified in the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> occurred as a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/de-novo/"><i>de novo</i></a> event in the proband or as a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/postzygotic/">postzygotic</a> <i>de novo</i> event in a mosaic parent [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.j_nsson.2017.519">J&#x000f3;nsson et al 2017</a>]. If the proband appears to have <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/homozygous/">homozygous</a> pathogenic variants (i.e., the same two pathogenic variants), additional possibilities to consider include:</div><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>A single- or multiexon <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/deletion/">deletion</a> in the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a> that was not detected by <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/sequence-analysis/">sequence analysis</a> and that resulted in the artifactual appearance of homozygosity;</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Uniparental isodisomy for the parental <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> with the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> that resulted in homozygosity for the pathogenic variant in the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>.</div></li></ul></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Although individuals <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> are not at risk for A-T, their risk of developing cancer is increased compared to that of the general population. In particular, heterozygous females younger than age 50 years have an increased risk of developing breast cancer (see Clinical Description, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes">Heterozygotes</a>); intensified breast cancer screening is recommended for heterozygous females (see Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Sibs of a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>If both parents are known to be <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, each sib of an affected individual has at conception a 25% chance of inheriting <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> pathogenic variants and having A-T, a 50% chance of inheriting one pathogenic variant and being heterozygous, and a 25% chance of inheriting neither of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/familial/">familial</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Sibs with <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/biallelic/">biallelic</a> <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants may show some differences in clinical presentation and laboratory findings (including immunologic abnormalities); however, the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/phenotype/">phenotype</a> within families tends to be similar [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.taylor.2015.199">Taylor et al 2015</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Although individuals <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> are not at risk for A-T, their risk of developing cancer is increased compared to that of the general population. In particular, heterozygous females younger than age 50 years have an increased risk of developing breast cancer (see Clinical Description, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes">Heterozygotes</a>); intensified breast cancer screening is recommended for heterozygous females (see Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</div></li></ul><p>
<b>Offspring of a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a></b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>Although most individuals with A-T do not reproduce, exceptions have been reported [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.stankovic.1998.334">Stankovic et al 1998</a>; <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.byrd.2012.262">Byrd et al 2012</a>; <a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.dawson.2015.1937">Dawson et al 2015</a>; Blancato et al, unpublished data]. Reproduction has been reported in several women with variant A-T [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.schon.2019.170">Schon et al 2019</a>].</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The offspring of an individual with A-T are obligate heterozygotes for a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> in <i>ATM</i> and are at increased risk for cancer (see Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</div></li></ul><p><b>Other family members.</b> Each sib of the <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/proband/">proband</a>'s parents is at a 50% risk of being <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> and at increased risk for cancer (see Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygote_Detecti"><h3>Heterozygote Detection</h3><p>Heterozygote testing for at-risk relatives requires prior identification of the <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants in the family.</p><p>Although individuals with a <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a> are not at risk for A-T, their risk of developing cancer is increased compared to that of the general population (see Clinical Description, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes">Heterozygotes</a>). In particular, heterozygous females have an increased risk of developing breast cancer, and intensified breast cancer screening is recommended [<a class="bk_pop" href="#ataxia-telangiectas.REF.van_os.2016.105">van Os et al 2016</a>] (see Surveillance, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Heterozygotes_1">Heterozygotes</a>).</p></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Related_Genetic_Coun"><h3>Related Genetic Counseling Issues</h3><p>See Management, <a href="#ataxia-telangiectas.Evaluation_of_Relati">Evaluation of Relatives at Risk</a> for information on evaluating at-risk relatives for the purpose of early diagnosis and treatment.</p><p>
<b>Family planning</b>
</p><ul><li class="half_rhythm"><div>The optimal time for determination of genetic risk and discussion of the availability of prenatal/<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> is before pregnancy.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div>It is appropriate to offer <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/genetic-counseling/">genetic counseling</a> (including discussion of potential risks to offspring and reproductive options) to young adults who are affected, are <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a>, or are at risk of being heterozygous.</div></li><li class="half_rhythm"><div><i>ATM</i> genetic testing should be considered for the reproductive partners of individuals known to be <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygous/">heterozygous</a> for an <i>ATM</i> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/pathogenic-variant/">pathogenic variant</a>, particularly if both partners are of the same ancestral background. Increased <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/heterozygote/">heterozygote</a> frequencies have been reported in the following populations due to founder variants: the Druze population in northern Israel, the Moroccan and Tunisian Jewish population, and the Romani population in Spain (see <a href="/books/NBK26468/table/ataxia-telangiectas.T.atm_pathogenic_var/?report=objectonly" target="object" rid-ob="figobataxiatelangiectasTatmpathogenicvar">Table 6</a>).</div></li></ul></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Prenatal_Testing_and"><h3>Prenatal Testing and Preimplantation Genetic Testing</h3><p>Once the <i>ATM</i> pathogenic variants have been identified in an affected family member, prenatal and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a> are possible.</p><p>Differences in perspective may exist among medical professionals and within families regarding the use of prenatal and <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/preimplantation-genetic-testing/">preimplantation genetic testing</a>. While most health care professionals would consider use of prenatal and preimplantation genetic testing to be a personal decision, discussion of these issues may be helpful.</p></div></div><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.Resources"><h2 id="_ataxia-telangiectas_Resources_">Resources</h2><p>

ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">Serine-protein kinase ATM</a></td><td rowspan="1" colspan="1" style="vertical-align:top;"><a style="vertical-align:top;"><a href="http://www.hgmd.cf.ac.uk/ac/gene.php?gene=ATM" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ATM</a></td><td rowspan="1" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">ATM</a></td></tr></tbody></table></div><div><div><dl class="temp-labeled-list small"><dt></dt><dd><div id="ataxia-telangiectas.TFA.1"><p class="no_margin">Data are compiled from the following standard references: <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/gene/">gene</a> from
<a href="http://www.genenames.org/index.html" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">HGNC</a>;
<a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/chromosome/">chromosome</a> <a class="def" href="/books/n/gene/glossary/def-item/locus/">locus</a> from
<a href="http://www.omim.org/" ref="pagearea=body&amp;targetsite=external&amp;targetcat=link&amp;targettype=uri">OMIM</a>;
